ParlaMint-AT_1998-11-27-020-XX-NRSITZ-00150_d7e2086	Federal Minister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_1998-11-27-020-XX-NRSITZ-00150_d7e2102	Ladies and gentlemen! I would like to point this out. However, the Austrian banking supervisory system – in so far as I am right – needs to be changed and strengthened due to the development of information technology, which repeatedly creates new types of banking operations and thus new risks, as well as liberalisation and globalisation. Such preparatory work was already started in the spring of 1998. With the creation of an organisation concept, an external consulting firm will be commissioned, whose first results will be available in mid-January. As a result of the current events, we will speed up work on the reform of banking supervision, because it is important to clearly indicate, even in the field of trust-building measures, that the system of banking supervision needs to be adapted accordingly, although the real causes in the Rieger case have to be determined only by court proceedings. The aim of our and my considerations is to make banking supervision in Austria more decisive. The structure should be tighter and more efficient and there should be more operational possibilities. In the medium term, as I believe, the allocation from the Ministry of Finance is necessary. Experience with the already distributed and fully operational securities supervision since the beginning of the year will be included here. The various components from which the Austrian system of banking supervision is composed must be taken into account. The role and responsibility such as the management, the internal revision of a bank, the auditor, of course also the Supervisory Board, of course also the banking supervision in the Ministry and the Oesterreichische Nationalbank, but also the courts must be defined precisely. We will also examine closely the various proposals, including the Chamber of Economic Trustees, the Chamber of Workers or the Austrian National Bank, and also look for the international comparison. Nevertheless, I would also like to say that, even with the best legal framework, criminal behaviour of market participants will not be excluded.

Both systems, in my humble impression, have advantages and disadvantages, and I believe that it is therefore important and makes sense to entrust to those consulting areas which we externally grant the task of looking at the systems in the other partner countries of the European Union in order to develop a system that is today characterised by optimal functionality.

As I have done, we have done very important pioneering work, and I am therefore very confident that the work of the Austrian Presidency – including in the field of ECOFIN – will be largely positive among our partner countries. And that seems to me to be of great importance for the reputation of our country.
ParlaMint-AT_1998-11-27-020-XX-NRSITZ-00150_d7e3018	Would you like an additional question? - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_1998-11-27-020-XX-NRSITZ-00150_d7e3033	In this context, what role does the demand for real expenditure stabilisation made by many Member States play?
ParlaMint-AT_1998-11-27-020-XX-NRSITZ-00150_d7e3048	Please, Mr. Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_1998-11-27-020-XX-NRSITZ-00150_d7e3064	This is a very important question, a question which will no longer be decided under the Austrian EU Presidency. In the context of the Agenda 2000 decision-making, the aim is to develop key values which form the basis for the medium-term financial perspective for the years 2000 to 2006. This is, of course, a very sensitive question, because, as a result of the structure of the European Union, there are countries that contribute more to the common mission of the European Union than others; there are more rich and poorer Member States. In principle, however, it must be considered that there must be reasonableness in terms of payments to the European Union in relation to the return flows and that this should be maintained to a reasonable extent. The discussion in this regard took place in Europe when Austria took over the presidency, in the range between the main "net payers" and an arbitrary correction mechanism, as well as the "net recipients" and funding via GNP per capita. In that thesis or antithesis, this discussion took place. This initiative, originally adopted by four countries of Europe, namely to state that real expenditure stabilisation is also in line with the principle of national fiscal consolidation, has succeeded in achieving convergence of the individual budgets in 11 Member States. This principle must also be based on the financing of European Union programmes. I can see here that in terms of real expenditure stabilisation over the last few weeks – Austria has more to do with the function of a moderator than with the Presidency – a very clear majority has been crystallised out, and in this context, of course, also the inflation rates that do not remain the same. But through this stabilisation, certain margins for financing additional tasks can be ensured.

ParlaMint-AT_1998-11-27-020-XX-NRSITZ-00150_d7e3139	Federal Minister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_1998-11-27-020-XX-NRSITZ-00150_d7e3154	First: You, honourable Members, did not pay close attention to this. But that's no wonder, because when your name was called up by Mr President, you were just talking to someone. My expression "pioneer work" was not referring to tax harmonisation, but to the very concrete forms of the development of economic dialogue. – You can read it in the minutes. We have indeed been pioneering, because the economic dialogue, which was actually called into question at the beginning of our EU Presidency by many relevant large countries, has thus started. To date, it has been questioned whether it is really necessary to engage the European Central Bank in the economic dialogue between the 11 Member States or with the involvement of the European Central Bank. In the meantime, please do not dispute that this economic policy dialogue among these eleven countries – which have different responsibilities for each other, precisely because of the single currency – also has to be extended to fiscal policy. And it is now also beyond dispute that this dialogue must also be conducted with the European Central Bank, which is of absolute necessity – without wishing to call into question the principle of independence of the central banks. Dialogue does not mean questioning independence, but dialogue means: fair efforts to come up with common solutions in the interest of the 11 Member States and the common currency. As far as tax harmonisation is concerned, I would like to point out that the pan-European tax discussion has been taking place since 1991 as far as harmonisation is concerned. The so-called Ruding Report pointed out what development the European Union could take if there was a more contradictive development of the individual tax systems. That harmonisation is extremely difficult, as there are different national tax systems in 11 European countries, there is no need for particular emphasis. The fact that, as I believe, the Austrian Presidency will certainly be extremely positive in this area is that until last year, we saw selectively the tax policy, the steps of harmonisation or coordination – once this area and once that – and we were surprised that we did not actually go further, while, also from the pan-European tax conference that we carried out at the beginning of our EU Presidency in Vienna with experts from all the countries of the European Union and beyond, we have come to think that each country of the European Union has certain aspects of its national tax system that irritates other areas of the single market. We have therefore come to think that we have at all an opportunity to adapt the various systems gradually, if this is presented in a package and then we take the lead in the discussion to demonstrate that everyone, as it were, must move at another end of such a coordinated package. For this question too, with very few exceptions; if I say that all 15 Member States already applaud it, a vast majority in relation to such a procedure has been achieved. By the way: I have always said that tax harmonisation is not a matter of six months but a longer-term process. We have therefore talked about this before we tackle this problem, both with Germany and with Finland – these are the two countries that we believe will have the EU Presidency. And only after Germany and Finland have been of this opinion – yes, this is an important issue, and that becomes even more important when there is the common currency – have we started this work. – As this will then be judged, we will see. There is still a little to be expected of how the assessment of our partners in the European Union will be.

[[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! The new asylum law, which was adopted in 1997, was also very much discussed at the time of its introduction. It has been said by various sides of an intensification and of the fact that the new asylum law will just reach the opposite of what it should achieve, namely those people who are actually persecuted to ensure access to a proper asylum procedure. However, previous practice has shown that all these fears did not come true, and many of the improvements we introduced at the time are now very much supported by the then critics. For example, we have introduced an independent federalasylsenate, a second instance. I can still remember how this was discussed, how it was criticized, how above all the occupation of the President was discussed and criticized by the Greens and Liberals. I think this is very important, and we can be very proud of it together. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] At the time, however, we also improved the third state clause, ladies and gentlemen. So far, it has always been the case that the third-country clause has, in some way, been regarded as a past forecast, i.e. whether in the past someone was safe from persecution in the relevant third country. At the time, we have determined that it is not about the past, but about the future that is crucial to what will happen to people in third countries in the future. I think that was important, too. And we have also set out, for example, the recognition of sexual violence against women on political grounds as grounds of asylum. That is why I believe, ladies and gentlemen, that the new asylum law, as we decided at the time, was an important, courageous step, and that this is a law that has also proved successful in practice. It is also true, Mr Kier, what I announced at the time: that we are observing this new asylum law for a year and that after a year together - the competent authorities, as well as the non-governmental organizations and the political parties - we are trying to draw from the experience the necessary consequences, the necessary consequences in improving the practice, in improving execution, but also, if necessary, we will introduce legal changes. I am still committed to this, and I have already begun the dialogue with the non-governmental organizations. ==References====External links== ==References== We have agreed that further talks will take place in January next year. I have received a number of reports from non-governmental organisations, and we will conduct this dialogue – I promise you to lead very seriously – and we will also present to Parliament the common results that come out of it for any change in the law. Ladies and gentlemen! It has already been said about the need for this novelle today. I do not want to repeat, but believe that it is necessary and important that we take account of the statement of the Constitutional Court. And with regard to the subject of the shortened appeal procedure, Mrs Petrovic – which seems important to me – says: They represent this two-day period as if it had been the case in any asylum procedure, and that it has always been a decision on life and death. That's not it! The two-day period has been introduced exclusively in the shortened appeal procedure and in those procedures in which there is obvious unfoundedness of the asylum procedure. Only there, not in the normal asylum procedures! And we have now done more than the Constitutional Court asks us to do, namely not a seven-day deadline, but a ten-day period in order to be able to shut down the weekend. We have also taken into account the suggestion of the Administrative Court to give the Bundesasylsenat an additional deadline for the in-depth examination. This additional period should not be more than 20 days. I believe that we can represent this, because it is also about supporting the asylum seekers. It is inhuman if an asylum seekers has to wait more than 20 days in the transit room at the airport for a decision whether to be admitted to an asylum procedure or not. We should try to avoid that. Ladies and gentlemen! And what's important, in my opinion, is: There will continue to be a case-by-case check in each procedure. This has been the case in the past, and this is also an important principle for the future after the new novel. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! Third-country security can only exist if certain conditions are met. I would like to mention these conditions once again; they are clearly regulated in the law, and there will also be a clear regulation in this respect in the coming regulation of the Minister of the Interior. First of all, it must be ensured that the applicant in the State concerned is safe from deportation against the country of origin, second, that in the State in which he receives his proceedings he receives a fair asylum procedure in accordance with the directives of the Geneva Convention on Refugees, and third, that he is allowed to stay in that State during the procedure and is not relocated to his State of origin. And, my dear ones. Ladies and gentlemen, this safe third country must have ratified the Geneva Convention on Refugees and the European Convention on Human Rights, and it must have set up an asylum procedure in accordance with the principles of the Geneva Convention on Refugees, which has a possibility of appeal, with an individual complaint to the Human Rights Court in Strasbourg. All of these are the necessary criteria and conditions for a state to be regarded as a safe third country. I think my dear ones. Ladies and gentlemen, this is a version of the third country security that exists in Europe only in a few countries. Many other European countries, whether that is Germany or whether it is Sweden, whether it is the Netherlands or the UK, have no interpretation of third-country security as much as Austria. I'll bet you do that. I think this is necessary and important because it must be our objective that the people who are persecuted politically also have the opportunity to find a state in which they are safe from persecution and in which they have the opportunity to build a new existence. This basic concept, ladies and gentlemen, does not in any way change anything with this novel to the asylum law, quite the contrary: This novelle serves to consolidate and support this basic concept. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] I would also like to say something very clearly: This mandate to the Minister of the Interior is one that I will take very seriously, and I will not make it easy for each individual decision on whether or not a State is a third-country-proof or not, but will act very conscientiously and in accordance with strict rule of law. I can assure you that I will implement this regulation only after a fundamental, detailed opinion, that I will obtain an opinion from the Federal Ministry of Foreign Affairs, that I will draw up a detailed questionnaire for each individual state on the necessary, important issues for third-country security, that I will also intend to involve the UNHCR in the decision, and that I will make it before a decision: Third-country security – yes or no? will also listen. I think it is important and necessary to stress that this will not be an autonomous decision by the Minister of the Interior alone, but rather a decision carried out by an appropriate opinion, carried out by the agreement of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs and a decision which will be taken under the supervision of the UNHCR. In my opinion, this is much better than if, in the case of 14 000 asylum applications we currently have per year, the authority has to decide again each case, an official has to decide on each case whether the person concerned in Hungary, in the Czech Republic or in Switzerland is third-country-proof. It is much more reasonable for the Minister of the Interior to be authorized by regulation to make a fundamental decision here, which can be called into question at any time when the political situation in that State changes. This basic regulation is based on recommendations from the UNHCR, based on a legal opinion of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs and is secured by a broad consensus. Ladies and gentlemen! I would like to stress once again that asylum law is an important human right for me and that we have the obligation to do everything we can to ensure that the right of asylum for the people who are persecuted is safeguarded in the future. At the same time, however, I would like to stress that there is no right to asylum in a particular state, but only the right to obtain asylum in a state in which one is safe from persecution. It cannot be that an asylum-seeker chooses his State for social standards or other considerations. It must be decisive that we in Europe do not become a fortress, but that we remain open in Europe to asylum seekers who are persecuted politically, religiously or ethnicly. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] I would also like to clear up with an error, Mrs Petrovic: that all the people who apply for asylum are persecuted solely for political, religious or ethnic reasons and that is why they are seeking asylum. Unfortunately, it is all too often the case that the asylum application is made in order to achieve immigration. Too often, the asylum application is also misused. I would like to mention a specific number from 1998. We have 10 700 asylum applications at the end of October this year. We haven't decided about that yet. We have decided 371 positive and 2,676 negative. This is an recognition rate of 12.2 percent, which makes us significantly above the international average, clearly above the EU average. But: 700 people have withdrawn their asylum applications, and in the first ten months alone, 3,500 people have applied for asylum, have been cared for by us – and have suddenly disappeared. We cannot continue to deal with asylum applications because these people have disappeared somewhere. That is one of our problems, and that is also the problem we have with the Federal Service. If we were to introduce federal care as a legal claim for any asylum seekers, that would mean that anyone who is illegally crossing the border would, of course, automatically apply for asylum, because he then has the guarantee of federal care. Austria is one of the few countries that have a significant increase in the number of asylum seekers vis-à-vis other countries. On the recognition rate, I would also like to say clearly that Austria is a leader in the international field. I was in Norway recently. The rich Norway, in many areas comparable to us, has had nearly 2,000 asylum applications last year. Of these 2,000, 85 last year – in absolute terms: 85! – asylum applications are decided positively. I could continue at any time. Italy has had 2 000 asylum applications last year, Spain 4 900, Finland 1 000 asylum applications, Portugal 250. There is hardly a state, measured by the number of inhabitants who have more asylum applications than Austria. After Germany and Switzerland, we are the third state, together with the Netherlands, with regard to the number of asylum seekers. I think we can also find the number of recognitions in the top three. I am proud of it, and I believe that we should not ignore this development together, but rather call it positive. ParlaMint-AT_1998-11-27-020-XX-NRSITZ-00150_d7e6000	Mr President. Federal Minister! My very much appreciated Ladies and gentlemen! High house! There is hardly any subject that, with such regularity, creates hot discussions, such as the issue of refugees and asylum policy. Finding a proper legal regime on the question of foreigners is like hiking. On the one hand, there is broad consensus among the population that it is impossible to keep the borders open to all immigrants. But on the other hand, each individual gift can be seen in such a way that not only those concerned are often displeased. The number of asylum seekers in Austria is increasing steadily. Mr Federal Minister, you mentioned the figures here earlier. Some of the main causes are the crisis in Kosovo and the economic situation in Eastern Europe and in other countries. Austria and Germany are most affected by immigration. However, they should not have to bear the main burden, so a distribution of refugees across Europe is essential. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! The occasion of today's amendment to the Asylum Act is a recognition of the Constitutional Court. In July, the latter abolished § 32, which provides for a two-day term of appeal, as unconstitutional. In practice, this regulation has made it impossible to provide technical assistance within this short period of time and to make a proper appeal. Today's change gives asylum seekers a 10-day term of appeal. The basis for this is experience from the current 14-day term of appeal. The reduction to ten days, apart from the period in general administrative procedure, is therefore to be welcomed. In practice, it has also been found that appeals are mostly brought on the last day. An unnecessary delay in the asylum procedure, which neither helps the State nor the refugee, has been removed. A further change is the setting of the decision-making period of the independent federalasylsenate on ten working days and in justified cases even for a total of up to 20 days. As a result of this new regulation, it must be possible for the independent Bundesasylsenate to establish a true and comprehensive case-finding. In line with the previous practical experience of the independent Bundesasylsenate, the maximum limit to a maximum of 20 working days is the most effective. The most controversial point in this amendment concerns the way in which safe third countries are laid down by law. This has led to intense discussions in recent weeks. I would have had nothing to do with the original procedure of the Federal Minister, namely to define the safe third country by law. In view of the fact that all the countries concerned are candidates for accession to the EU, I think that would be useful. The adoption by the Federal Minister's regulation is a way of the middle, a compromise with which we should all be satisfied. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] It will be important when you, Federal Minister, make the regulations. I cannot follow the criticism that it is more or less a means of eliminating the constitutionally guaranteed right to asylum – as is seen by the Liberals and the Greens. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] If an applicant claims that he has no safe protection in a safe third country covered by the regulation due to a situation in which he is in his person, the authority shall be required to carry out an individual examination. The personal right of each individual person to asylum is therefore not affected. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Mr President. Those who believe that it will be less difficult to control immigration without hardship in the future will be very wrong. Prosperity and jobs in the EU are so tempting that people from poorer countries take every opportunity to come to and from the EU and thus also to Austria. This will not change for so long as people in their home countries cannot gain better living conditions. In order to return to the above mentioned ridges: I think we have succeeded in doing this with today's novelle to the Asylum Act! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] [[Intrusion of the Abg. Dr. Pumberger.]] This novel is a way of the middle, and I am therefore happy to give my consent.

As a result, when it comes to larger repair work in a house, 40 percent less money is available and the tax performance of homeowners is transferred directly to the tenants, because the tenants have to pay the costs of the renovation. We have decided in 1997 by means of the Law on Rental Law that there should be other forms of time-limitation for residential purposes, that there is the possibility, for example, to rent for a ten-year period. At the same time, however, in the Fees Act, we have not amended the rules on this subject, according to which the fee is either 1 percent of the monthly rents over the period of time or a maximum of three years. This is the result of the fact that, at present, a tenant – which is why I am glad that we decide today – when he completes a temporary lease contract for ten years, has to pay 1 percent of the ten annual rents and someone who completes an indefinite rental contract – we agree that this is for the tenant of the more valuable rental contract – to pay 1 percent of three year rents. The current situation is unfair, and I am glad that we also decide on this ceiling today for temporary rental contracts. If you want, we can also see this as a first step in the right direction with regard to the remuneration of rental contracts. However, I would also like to comment on the question of why there is no analogy in business space rental. The question of the possibilities of time limits for renting business premises is quite different from that of rental apartments. I believe that the group of people affected by temporary rental contracts is not comparable to that of commercial rental. I would also like to point out that the tenant bears the remuneration of the rental contract, but the remuneration of business premises rental contracts can be depreciated as operating expenses. I think that this too must be an aspect of deciding on this change today, which is an improvement for all tenants who enter into a temporary rental relationship. That is why I am glad to have decided today on these amendments and on these reforms. We can gladly agree with this whole thing. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_1998-11-27-020-XX-NRSITZ-00150_d7e10339	Mr President. Mr. State Secretary! The fact is that the current financial reform law disbands a financial office. I believe that further follow-up will be followed, and I will explain this on the basis of four points. First point: The following are to be found in the explanatory notes: In the interests of a federal uniform system of jurisdiction, it is also envisaged that all financial offices with extended responsibilities at the head office of the Financial Directorate should also be responsible for the major Gesmbhs. I take as an example the financial office Weiz and the Elin plant in Weiz. I think Elin has about 1,500 employees, the headquarters of the management will be in Vienna. In other words, the entire wage tax revenue of the Finanzamt Weiz will be charged to Vienna, and the Finanzamt and the Elin-Werke in Weiz will also be examined by the Vienna wage taxpayers. The wage tax revenue in the Weiz Financial Office will therefore be reduced by about half. The Finanzamt Weiz will lose in category size, it will become a Tripartite or Vieramt, it will become a Small Office. It will be similar with many financial offices outside the big cities. As a second point, I suggest that the regionalisation concept for the operational tests is not yet out of the table. The operational audits should also be grouped into larger units, withdrawn from the small financial offices and allocated to larger financial offices. This is the second step in getting to smaller units so that the categories change accordingly. Third point: The Tax Reform Commission has proposed to delete the admissibility of the expenditure on pots. If these potting expenditures are eliminated, the payroll tax offices will be reduced by about 60 percent. These measures will also reduce the financial offices in the country accordingly. As a fourth point, I suggest that there are self-esteems in some areas. This will also be decided today by the tax-amending law, i.e. even by these self-esteems officials will be deducted from the small financial offices. That is why I am going to refuse to accept this Financial Reform Act. Mr State Secretary! I would ask you to help ensure that in future regional offices or public institutions remain in the regions. Gendarmerie posts have already been dissolved. It will be so at the post offices, and at the courts we will still experience it. So you clear the countryside and move everything into the big cities. I will therefore not agree to this draft law. Thank you.

If we enter the euro area if we can already put our balance sheets in euro from next year onwards – this will be mandatory from 2002 onwards – it is about comparing Austrian business balance sheets in the European environment. They should be comparable in order to allow Austrian companies, for example, to receive loans in other EU countries. Today, if you, as an Austrian entrepreneur, want to receive a loan from a foreign bank, in many cases you have to provide a package of evaluation opinions with your balance sheet in order to demonstrate that the existing approaches in the balance sheet – which are historically based on the acquisition values that come from a time many decades before – are no longer in agreement, so that the capital resources of the company are actually much better. - It does, please, no sense! We took the corresponding step in Austria in 1954 and 1955 respectively. It was Finance Minister Kamitz who enforced this: a schilling opening balance sheet to bring back the ball-horned balance sheets of Austrian companies after the Second World War, where they belong in reality. In the 1980s and 1990s, large companies over box constructions, holding structures against this deficit – this undervaluation imposed by the Commercial Code – brought this undervaluation through sales – into real balance sheet creation. Only very few stock companies that have gone to the stock exchange in recent years could have gone to the stock exchange without this measure, because they had been undercapitalised in the last few years. The small and medium-sized enterprises in our country have always been blocked. That is why I address once again the request to you, ladies and gentlemen of the House, to the question of a millennium opening balance or a century opening stock – whatever you want to call it; I have just referred to it as "Euro opening balance", but in reality it is indifferent as you call it – to turn it to and say: Let us give the small and medium-sized enterprises in Austria the opportunity, upon submission of opinions, to upgrade both their basic assets and their movable assets and their movable assets! To that end, we need to open the strict low-value principle in accordance with the Commercial Code for three years in order to enable these upgrades to be made legally possible. In addition, of course, we also need to introduce an upgrade tax. In other words, we need to remove our proposal after 10 percent of the unmovable and 15 percent of the moveable assets for these gains – not for land that they are not depreciable, but for the movable and movable assets. Then, of course, the company has a cash flow – no question at all – but it can neutralise this cash outflow due to the increased depreciation base over the next 3, 4 or 5 years – depending on the profit situation. The effect of this would be and is that small and medium-sized enterprises working in a larger economic and monetary area in the future can represent their balance sheets as they really are in terms of content. I think that this measure is very useful, and I am pleased to be able to tell you that at the first reading we were reading over a year ago – on 19 November. September 1997 – when some of them were still concerned, the speakers of the other groups of the House made a positive statement on the matter at the time. If you agree with this appreciation, this is, of course, an appreciation in the trade balance, which affects the balance of payments, because we want to maintain the principle that the trade balance is important for the balance of payments. The proposed measure would even bring together both balance sheets in some areas. So we should discuss the details, including the recovery gains. With 15 per cent, I consider the upgrading tax to be mobile and 10 per cent unmovable investment assets to be useful. We have always described it as a goal that should not be upgraded to more than the purchase values. In the case of plots, transport values should be used after advice has been drawn up. It is clear that, of course, an upgrading – especially of land – has to be carried out in return for recovery. This revaluation will then be dissolved over the course of 10 years in order to prevent someone from upgrading, then sold and thus collected the full recovery profit. Such a provision is also possible and without involving major accounting measures. Mrs Frieser found the name questionable. We agree; then we call it "thousandth-century opening stock". The goal remains the same: To represent values in the balance sheet that are realistic, but not what we are doing today and what you can see in national banking statistics where capital resources are in the minus range. I would also like to point out that in many cases this corresponds to reality, but let us give the companies that are healthy the opportunity to represent their real equity. With reference to your statement of reasons for today's deadline application, it should be said that the first part of your statement actually concerns a matter to be regulated by a company closing law, as is currently being prepared in the Federal Ministry of Justice. There is, in principle, no objection either. As far as the second part is concerned, we have already debated this at first reading, I do not think that the situation has changed very much since our last debate. For there has been no change in EU law since our last debate, we agree on this. That is to say, it is not permitted to upgrade to the transport value provided for here, in accordance with current EU legislation. That is the second point. Tax law, of course, makes it possible for such an appreciation from the point of view of EU law. We are still faced with the problem of an even stronger or more complete breakdown of trade and tax balances if we consider this to be permissible for tax purposes. However, we do not want to do so because we believe that the principle of trade balance should continue to apply to the balance of payments. In my view, therefore, there would be an increase in the administrative burden which is not desired from our side. As regards the fixed rate of taxation as provided for in your draft, the question arises as to its level. You have made a proposal to this effect. I would point out that we normally have tax rates up to 50 percent in the income tax sector and 34% in the corporate tax sector. That is to say, the question arises as to how the uniform rate of taxation you propose in the context of the principle of justice is taken into account. As regards the technical question of implementation, the question arises: Do you want an object tax to be paid regardless of the actual income? Or is it only a special tax rate in the context of the structure of income determination at all? – If one does not seek the first, but the second way, then of course it would also have to be clarified, as in the case of losses arising from current business operations, losses from other sources of income and losses in the context. They have partly dealt with this question. The last point – that is always one of the relevant points – is the question of tax revenue arising from it, is raised or reduced. It is true that, in the short term, an increase in revenue would be the result; that is beyond doubt. But according to our calculations, this increase would be far superior in later periods by much higher volume. This, of course, raises the question – outside the discussions that have already taken place on tax reform, on reliefs and charges – whether such relief constitutes a sensible measure. We believe that the argument that we made at the first reading has not changed substantially, and will not give our approval today to this proposal for a time limit.

ParlaMint-AT_1998-11-27-020-XX-NRSITZ-00150_d7e11575	The debate so far has actually shown, on all the concerns raised in part, that there has not been anyone who believes that this is not a really important matter and is also in need of discussion. It would be time for us to open the talks on the possibilities of a euro balance-sheet law - I am now using the name of our name - that we are discussing these possibilities as soon as possible, because that balance-sheet is a multiple wish, has already been emphasized by my previous speakers. If this wish were not to be fulfilled, because perhaps it would fail by the majority of the government parties or for whatever reason, then it would be beneficial if it could be discussed in committee as soon as possible, because such a balance sheet would be a solution to some of the problems our companies have. Companies should therefore know as soon as possible whether this instrument is given to them or not. I think that they should be given, because some of the arguments mentioned here are quite correct, because when we bring these arguments together, they prove once again that it is urgently necessary to have such a balance sheet law. The lowest value principle has a twin sister, that is the balance sheet truth. And the balance sheet truth is occasionally protected by the lowest value principle, so that one does not artificially become richer than one is. However, if the valuations are as much apart as is partly the case, because dynamic effects in the asset development in the balance sheet do not reflect and do not become visible, for example because the assets have been in the company for a long time and have not been acquired recently at higher prices, and because the internal revaluation effects are not visible in the balance sheet, this shows a distorted balance sheet view. This is not a problem for the company itself, because the entrepreneurs know their own silent reserves, but when someone enters the international stage and shows this balance, it is not very helpful if he has to argue with silent reserves. First of all, he suspects that he's only "something told", and second, it's not possible, because the silent reserves are published, not the balance sheets. So if someone wants to know about a competitor, about a potential business partner or otherwise, then he resorts to the published balance sheets and not to the documentation of the silent reserves in the company. Sometimes you are out of a store before you have noticed that you could have entered the store. But because the potential partner has made himself smart, but of course only knows the published balance sheet, the project was no longer carried out at all. In particular, our export-oriented economy may lose so many business without anyone noticing it. That's the way it is. But I think that if, in the sense of balance-sheet truth, we were to apply some of the lowest value-added principle in the balance-sheets, but not historically, then we could do something. If, at the same time, one introduced the instrument of a re-evaluation reserve so that no tax shock waves could pass over the company, so if one could flatten it, then the concern that trade balances and tax balances could differ too much would be fixable. It is precisely the objective of this assessment reserve that trade balances and tax balances are re-established equally over a certain period of time. This is very important, because for the public in the broadest sense of the word – that is, I mean the qualified audience of the potential business partners – the balance sheet truth is the key to assessing a balance sheet. It would be very nice if the government parties could jump over their shadows today and agree to the deadline request. Second best solution: The chairman of the Financial Committee shall simply take this item on the agenda. That would also be a possibility. When do we have to put forward proposals for deadlines? – If you have waited patiently for a long time, if you have all confirmed that the issue is important, and if it never appears on the agenda of the committee responsible, then you as the opposition party are simply drowned and forced to make appropriate requests for deadlines. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_1998-11-27-020-XX-NRSITZ-00150_d7e12620	Mr President. Mr. State Secretary! High house! At the first reading of the request or of the two amendments tabled, with which the Federal Budget Act is amended, one would like to agree because a few reasonable proposals are contained in it. The one proposal to amend § 62 aims to ensure that the financial system can also waive non-recoverable claims. That is to say, the proposal makes sense in so far as it often does not seem justified to be against a debtor who is economically very bad to carry out a process, so that it is said that one estimates it economically so that the success is dubious, one probably wins the process, but one does not get any money, and that is why it has failed to give up the demand, because the reduced rather higher process costs. We cannot agree with the second part, Mr State Secretary, in any way, because the following sentence has been added to this amendment – amended § 65c (1): Credit operations for other legal entities shall not be treated as financial debts of the Federal Government. What's that about? – It is practically a legitimacy for debt in the grey area. That means that it will become less and less transparent: Areas are distributed, the Federal Government continues to assume liability for this, but it is no longer included in the financial debt, which means that the debt picture is distorted. The Advisory Board on Economic and Social Affairs also criticised this and demanded that the financial debt of the federal government or even the state debt of the federal government be more transparent. This is not just the financial debt that is charged with meeting the Maastricht criteria, because debt remains to blame, and the liabilities remain for the federal government. It is also necessary to give an account of how administrative debt or the so-called supplier debt looks like, so that we can finally make a cash crash and know what the state really has in terms of liabilities. But you always go the way to outsource the federal government's financial debt so that you get closer and closer to the Maastricht criteria, but this distorts the picture of balance sheet truth. A very simple example: In the past it was with the banks that, when a loan was in need, one raised a property, including all process costs, all interest on default, and all late payment rates. This was then distributed into a subsidiary and therefore did not change the regular operating result in the balance sheet. Then the consolidation rules came, and you had to consolidate, but then you felt the malaise. It would be the same with the federal budget: If you had to consolidate the federal budget, you would also have to reveal the actual debt, and that would be very close to the balance sheet truth. [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] Mr Finanzminister! It is also the so-called flexibilisation clause a basically reasonable proposal. With regard to the flexibilisation clause, the Minister of Finance may, by regulation, give individual heads of organisational units a certain margin of action, in the way that there are often additional expenditure to be covered by additional revenue. But if the additional expenditure in the one financial year or in the one budgetary year cannot be covered, then you have at least the next two years to do so. However, if savings are made, there is a so-called bonus. But you must not forget the following: This is an experiment, but in the present case it is a constitutional provision! I believe that, if there are no precise principles as to how to deal with savings, what this flexibilization clause should look like, it should really be a little more cautious to deal with constitutional provisions. There is nothing in there that should happen to the so-called overlap if an administrative unit achieves savings? It is only within that that perhaps a premium is to be granted for training. I believe that this should be a bit more concrete, for example in the form that a so-called overling, which is achieved, goes down to 50 percent to the Minister of Finance, remains 40 percent in the department and 10 percent is to be used for premiums or other training costs. That would be a concrete approach! What is contained in this flexibilization clause is simply too dirty, and it is certainly not appropriate to experiment with it. The basic approach would be right! It is only once after the control instruments – § 28 Rules of Procedure of the National Council – that you already have in your hands to take action. In it is quite decided inside: If a self-employed application is made, which leads to additional expenditure compared to the Federal Finance Act in the respective chapter, then appropriate operational proposals must be made. Unfortunately, this is not the case with the applications. We experience it again and again. You have a second instrument at your disposal, namely § 14 of the Federal Budget Act and in the forwarding of § 17 of the Federal Budget Act, where the cost-benefit analysis is mentioned. The Minister of Finance or the Federal Government is instructed to lay down guidelines for cost-benefit analysis. If you would or would have long established these guidelines for cost-benefit analysis, you do not need this flexibilisation clause. I know that this is difficult, but it is a matter of budgeting in the budget and not just after input analysis by trying to get everything possible to get tax revenue so that we can regulate the output. One should really depend on how to manage a place of business wisely, in accordance with the principles of economy and economy as set out in the Constitution. Mr State Secretary! If you would like to have an information about the § 14 Federal Budget Act, this is not possible. As an example, I refer to the Government template 933 of the supplements which provided the possibility of opting for the pre-tax deduction of credit card companies. In this context, we have asked how high the costs incurred by this government proposal are. We have also made a written request to the Federal Minister for Finance. It is in the law as follows: How high these expenditures are within the current budget each year is to be estimated. – However, the government template stated that this would not cost. We had to find that the Minister of Finance – a pity he is not here today – simply went beyond the whole of the answer to this question. In fact, there is less revenue in this area of at least 300 million schilling. You know that very well, Mr State Secretary, just like all of us here in this House. Parliament has the right to ask what financial impact on the budget or the individual budget chapter has on a legislative amendment or a legislative proposal. It is not possible to go beyond the content of the Federal Budget Act, in which clear rules are laid down on a so-called cost-benefit analysis or an assessment of the less-income or an assessment of a higher effort. It is this request or the two amendments may be a way in a particular direction, but first you should follow the legal provisions already contained in the Federal Budget Act, or act in accordance with these legal provisions, and then you can get approval here in the House for new legal provisions. But before that does not happen, and as long as the law so far is a dead and no applicable right, you cannot obtain any consent from our site for so long.

Mr State Secretary! In connection with these self-interests, I would also like to talk briefly about the Controlling Advisory Board. The Federal Minister did not answer the question of the experience of such advisory committees in the Committee. I believe that flexibilization will be hindered by the additional installation of such an advisory board, because these advisory committees will be appointed by those to be monitored, or by advisory committees which can only make recommendations which have been made unanimously, or by a advisory board which gives its own rules of procedure, which will require the approval of the respective budgetary authority and the Minister of Finance. Speaking in plain text, Mr State Secretary: Each institution shall give its own separate rules of procedure to the Advisory Board which controls it. – Mr State Secretary! That's paradox! This means that the desired flexibilization will be lifted or not at all effective. Mr State Secretary! The only way to improve the efficiency of the budget and staff controls is to draw up the federal budget according to clear objectives and no longer according to the system of continuation. This system leads to a high degree of prestige investment and, in addition, to expenditure that is made only because of the fact that the funds are available in the budget approaches and otherwise they were in danger of being reduced accordingly. With the current system – with or without the budget control scheme – it is important that the budget is not budgeted as a priority, and that, for example, there are no funds available for the Research Fund for 1999, although everyone talks about the importance of research and development tasks. – Mr State Secretary! I think you need to think about it. Finally, I would like to point out that this request for amendment of the Federal Budget Act is to be rejected on its own because this amendment of § 100 constitutes a retroactive change in the law. – This is a process that does not contribute to strengthening confidence in Austrian legislation. - Thank you.

I have to tell you one thing: This half billion or, if you want to, these net excesses in the amount of not even 150 million schillings – that is the amount that must be covered in some way by more revenue in the sense clarified by me – are only half the truth or, more precisely, only a small fraction of the whole truth. For the ladies and gentlemen who were in the Committee on Budgets will probably remember that the same committee has reported on so-called pre-taxes and has been taken note of by the majority of the two government groups. Pre-loads include – very simplifiedly said – things that could be subsumed under the sentence: Buy today, consume today – pay tomorrow or the day after tomorrow. Of course, we can keep budget surpluses very low if we say that we are not going into the budget overrun, but into the budget predisbursement. This means, for example, that you enter into a contract or make a purchase of funds in which the final or partial amounts become due only later, et cetera, pp. This is a relatively simple process, with which you can keep the excess relatively small, if you do so on the other hand, or if you are suffocating it in such a way. That is why you would like to say that in the first three quarters of this year, the pre-taxes are already in a different order, namely in the order of about 4.5 billion schilling – and the last quarter of 1998 is not yet in place. Experience and nature increase in the last quarter rather than fall. This is also logical and clear, because it is at the end of the year that we have a budget that is less good than perhaps at the beginning of the year. I want to tell you that it could be – next to this 2. Budgetary breach law, which we are dealing with today – to sum up predispositions in this year, which – I don't know yet; perhaps the Secretary of State knows; no, he can't know yet, there's no such thing – are in the order of perhaps 6, 7 or 8 billion schilling. Of course you can say: Well, 6 or 7 billion schilling in the order of the entire federal budget may not be beautiful, but also not so dramatic. However, I must tell you that in this way 1.25 trillion schillings, i.e. 1 250 billion schillings, have been running together over the last 10, 15, 20 years and that these pre-taxes have been burdening future budgets for more than 30 years in advance. By the year 2031, finance ministers – whoever sets them – will have to deal with these charges, they will have to finance, or in any way they will have to be severed. In order to be able to imagine a little better what this figure of 1.25 trillion means – about, for example, because I have not yet prepared the figures for this year, so I can only say them to you in about, but they vote up to or down to a few billion – I would like to say the following: In the coming year, 165 billion schillings will be due out of these predispositions – that is no longer a negligible sum – and in 2000 110 billion schillings will be due. Just imagine what would be if these pre-taxes were not available, how beautiful the budgets would look, how easy it could be to swung here, you could almost even achieve surpluses. I just want to say: You can't put this on your side and move it to the side by saying: All right, these are predispositions. One day, these charges will be financial debt and will burden us. Last sentence: These pre-taxes, which you are constantly making year after year, or better said, quarter by quarter, are one of the reasons why, despite tax packages and despite the efforts of your own and the Federal Government to clean up the budget, you are not really going to get along with it and, above all, are not sustainable, because the debt, the councils, the support contracts you are making today in order to beautify the respective budgets – see 1998 budget law or budget 1998 – you will recover tomorrow or tomorrow at the latest. Therefore, you will never be able to clean up a budget with this basic setting.
